\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{8}\tilde{\eta}_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}(\Delta u_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n})^{2}+\frac{1}{16}\tilde{\theta}_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}\lambda(\Delta u_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n})^{3}+\mathcal{O}(\Delta k_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}})

(6.44)

\displaystyle{\hskip 14.22636pt}+\mathcal{O}(k_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}-k_{j})+\mathcal{O}(k_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}-k_{j+1}),

(6.45)

with the coefficients
\tilde{\eta}_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n},\tilde{\theta}_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}\geq 0.

To achieve this, we begin with decomposing the term
\mathcal{A}_{3}
as

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{3}=\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{3}+\mathcal{O}(\Delta k_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}),

(6.46)

where we define

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{3}

\displaystyle:=\left[4\beta\frac{\sigma_{j+1}^{n}}{\Delta u_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}}f_{uu}(c_{5},\zeta_{5})-4\beta\frac{\sigma_{j}^{n}}{\Delta u_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}}f_{uu}(c_{4},\zeta_{4})\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+\left[\lambda(f_{uu}(c_{4},\zeta_{4}))(2\tilde{a}_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}-2a_{j}^{n})\left(\frac{\sigma_{j}^{n}}{\Delta u^{n}_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}}\right)\right.

\displaystyle{\hskip 14.22636pt}{\hskip 14.22636pt}\left.+\lambda f_{uu}(c_{5},\zeta_{5})(2a_{{j+1}}^{n}-2\tilde{a}_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n})\left(\frac{\sigma_{j+1}^{n}}{\Delta u^{n}_{j+\frac{1}{2}}}\right)\right].

Here, the term
\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{3}
can be bounded as

\displaystyle\lvert\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{3}\rvert

\displaystyle\leq 8\gamma_{2}(\lvert\beta\rvert+\kappa).

(6.47)

This is derived using (
2.3
) and the hypothesis
(H2)
, along with the fact that
0\leq\frac{\sigma_{j+1}^{n}}{\Delta u^{n}_{j+\frac{1}{2}}}\leq 1
(see (
3.2
)).
Using the same arguments, we obtain a bound on the term
\mathcal{A}_{4}
in (
6.39
) as

\displaystyle\lvert\mathcal{A}_{4}\rvert\leq C_{0}\lambda\lvert\Delta u_{j+\frac{1}{2}}^{n}\rvert\gamma_{2},

(6.48)

where
\displaystyle C_{0}:=\frac{1}{4}+\kappa+\frac{\kappa^{2}}{4}+\frac{1}{2}\lambda C_{u_{0}}\gamma_{2}.
In addition to this bound, we also need to split
\mathcal{A}_{4}
into a suitable form. To do this, we rewrite the term
\lambda N
in (
6.39
) by using the expressions for
f_{uu}(k_{j},\zeta_{2})
and
f_{uu}(k_{{j+1}},\zeta_{3})
taken from the Taylor expansions given in (
6.21
),

\displaystyle\lambda N=E_{1}+E_{2}+E_{3},

(6.49)

where

\displaystyle E_{1}

\displaystyle:=\lambda\frac{\left(f(k_{j+1},u_{j+1}^{n+\frac{1}{2}})-f(k_{j},u_{j}^{n+\frac{1}{2}})\right)}{\Delta u_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}},

(6.50)

\displaystyle E_{2}

\displaystyle:=-\lambda\frac{\left(f(k_{j+1},u_{j+1}^{n})-f(k_{j},u_{j}^{n})\right)}{\Delta u_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}},\,\,E_{3}:=\lambda^{2}\frac{\left(a_{j+1}^{2}(\sigma_{j+1}^{n})^{2}-a_{j}^{2}(\sigma_{j}^{n})^{2}\right)}{2\Delta u_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}}.

Adding and subtracting
\lambda f(k_{j},u_{j+1}^{n+\frac{1}{2}})
to the numerator and applying the mean value theorem, we may write
E_{1}
as

\displaystyle E_{1}

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathcal{O}(\Delta k_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}})}{\Delta u_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}}+\lambda f_{u}(k_{j},\zeta_{8})\left(1-\frac{\lambda}{2}\left(a_{j+1}^{n}\frac{\sigma_{j+1}^{n}}{\Delta u_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}}-a_{j}^{n}\frac{\sigma_{j}^{n}}{\Delta u_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}}\right)\right),

(6.51)

for some
c_{8}\in\mathcal{I}(k_{j},k_{j+1})
and
\zeta_{8}\in\mathcal{I}(u_{j}^{{n+\frac{1}{2}}},u_{j+1}^{{n+\frac{1}{2}}}),
where we have used the definition of
u_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{{n+\frac{1}{2}}}
from (
3.5
). Similarly, adding and subtracting
\lambda(f(k_{j},u_{j+1}^{n})),
we may write

E_{2}
as

\displaystyle E_{2}

\displaystyle=\lambda\frac{\mathcal{O}(\Delta k_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}})}{\Delta u_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}}-\lambda f_{u}(k_{j},\zeta_{9}),

(6.52)

for some
c_{9}\in\mathcal{I}(k_{j},k_{j+1})
and
\zeta_{9}\in\mathcal{I}(u_{j}^{n},u_{j+1}^{n}).

Now, substituting the expressions for
E_{1},E_{2}
and
E_{3}
from (
6.51
), (
6.52
) and (
6.50
), respectively, and using the relations (
6.27
) and (
6.35
) for the terms
f_{uu}(c_{4},\zeta_{4}),

f_{uu}(c_{5},\zeta_{5}),

f_{uu}(c_{6},\zeta_{6})
and
f_{uu}(c_{7},\zeta_{7}),

the term
\mathcal{A}_{4}
can be reformulated as

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{4}

\displaystyle=\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{4}+\frac{\mathcal{O}(\Delta k_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}})}{\Delta u_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}},

(6.53)

where
\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{4}:=\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{4}^{1}+\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{4}^{2}+\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{4}^{3}+\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{4}^{4}+\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{4}^{5}+\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{4}^{6},
and

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{4}^{1}

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{2}\lambda\left[f_{u}(k_{j+1},\zeta_{11})+f_{u}(k_{j},\zeta_{10})-2f_{u}(\tilde{k}_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}},u_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}))\right],

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{4}^{2}

\displaystyle:=-\lambda\beta\left[\frac{\sigma^{n}_{j+1}}{\Delta u_{j+\frac{1}{2}}^{n}}(a_{{j+1}}^{n}-\tilde{a}_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n})+\frac{\sigma^{n}_{j}}{\Delta u_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}}(\tilde{a}_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}-a_{j}^{n})\right],

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{4}^{3}

\displaystyle:=-\frac{1}{8}\lambda^{2}\left[\frac{\sigma_{j+1}^{n}}{\Delta u_{j+\frac{1}{2}}^{n}}\left(4(a_{j+1}^{n})^{2}+4(\tilde{a}_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n})^{2}-8a_{j+1}^{n}\tilde{a}_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+\frac{1}{8}\lambda^{2}\left[\frac{\sigma_{j}^{n}}{\Delta u_{j+\frac{1}{2}}^{n}}\left(4(a_{j}^{n})^{2}+4(\tilde{a}_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n})^{2}-8a_{j}^{n}\tilde{a}_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}\right)\right],

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{4}^{4}

\displaystyle:=\lambda f_{u}(k_{j},\zeta_{8})\left[1-\frac{\lambda}{2}\left(a_{j+1}^{n}\frac{\sigma_{j+1}^{n}}{\Delta u_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}}-a_{j}^{n}\frac{\sigma_{j}^{n}}{\Delta u_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}}\right)\right],

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{4}^{5}

\displaystyle:=-\lambda f_{u}(k_{j},\zeta_{9}),\quad\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{4}^{6}:=\lambda^{2}\frac{\left(a_{j+1}^{2}(\sigma_{j+1}^{n})^{2}-a_{j}^{2}(\sigma_{j}^{n})^{2}\right)}{2\Delta u_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}}.

Using (
2.3
), (
5.3
) and the fact that
0\leq\frac{\sigma_{j+1}^{n}}{\Delta u^{n}_{j+\frac{1}{2}}}\leq 1,
we easily obtain the bounds

\displaystyle\lvert\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{4}^{1}\rvert

\displaystyle\leq 2\kappa,\quad\lvert\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{4}^{2}\rvert\leq 4\kappa^{2},\quad\lvert\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{4}^{3}\rvert\leq 4\kappa^{2},\quad\lvert\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{4}^{4}\rvert\leq\kappa(1+\kappa),

\displaystyle\lvert\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{4}^{5}\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\kappa\quad\mbox{and}\quad\lvert\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{4}^{6}\rvert\leq 2C_{u_{0}}\kappa^{2},

and hence

\displaystyle\lvert\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{4}\rvert\leq C_{1}\kappa,

(6.54)

where
C_{1}:=4+(9+2C_{u_{0}})\kappa.
Upon rewriting (
6.43
) using the expressions (
6.46
) and (
6.53
) for
\mathcal{A}_{3}
and
\mathcal{A}_{4},
respectively, we arrive at

\displaystyle\theta_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}

\displaystyle=\tilde{\theta}_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}+\frac{\mathcal{O}(\Delta k_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}})}{(\Delta u_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n})^{2}},

(6.55)

where we define

\displaystyle\tilde{\theta}_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}

\displaystyle:=\mathcal{A}_{2}-\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{3}+16\frac{\beta\mathcal{A}_{4}}{\lambda\Delta u_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}}+8\frac{\mathcal{A}_{4}\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{4}}{\lambda\Delta u_{j+\frac{1}{2}}^{n}}.

(6.56)

Analogously, in view of (
6.53
), (
6.42
) yields

\displaystyle\eta_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}

\displaystyle=\tilde{\eta}_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}+\frac{\mathcal{O}(\Delta k_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}})}{\Delta u_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}},

where we define

\displaystyle\tilde{\eta}_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}

\displaystyle:=(1-4\beta^{2})\left[1-\frac{1}{16}(1-4\beta^{2})\left(\frac{\Delta\sigma_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}}{\Delta u_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}}\right)^{2}\right.

(6.57)

\displaystyle}{}{\displaystyle\left.-(\beta+\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{4})\left(\frac{\Delta\sigma_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}}{\Delta u_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}}\right)-\frac{(\sigma_{j}^{n}+\sigma_{j+1}^{n})}{2\Delta u_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}}\right],

Hence, we obtain the desired reformulation (
6.44
) of (
6.41
).

Next, we show that
\tilde{\eta}_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}\geq 0
and
\tilde{\theta}_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}>0,\,\mbox{for all}\,{j\in\mathbb{Z}}.

First, we consider the term
\tilde{\eta}_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n},

and note that

1-4\beta^{2}\geq 0,
by the CFL condition (
6.3
). Now, using the bound (
6.54
), a portion of the term
\tilde{\eta}^{n}_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}
can be bounded as

\displaystyle 1-\frac{1}{16}(1-4\beta^{2})\left(\frac{\Delta\sigma_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}}{\Delta u_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}}\right)^{2}-(\beta+\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{4})\left(\frac{\Delta\sigma_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}}{\Delta u_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}}\right)-\frac{(\sigma_{j}^{n}+\sigma_{j+1}^{n})}{2\Delta u_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}}

(6.58)

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\geq\left(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{16}(1-4\beta^{2})\lvert\frac{\Delta\sigma_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}}{\Delta u_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}}\rvert-\lvert\beta+\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{4}\rvert\right)\Big\lvert\frac{\Delta\sigma_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}}{\Delta u_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}}\Big\rvert+1

\displaystyle{\hskip 14.22636pt}{\hskip 14.22636pt}-\left(\Big\lvert\frac{\Delta\sigma_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}}{2\Delta u_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}}\Big\rvert+\frac{(\sigma_{j}^{n}+\sigma_{j+1}^{n})}{2\Delta u_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\geq\left(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{16}(1-4\beta^{2})-\lvert\beta+C_{1}\kappa\rvert\right)\Big\lvert\frac{\Delta\sigma_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}}{\Delta u_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}}\Big\rvert\geq 0.

Here, we use the bound
\displaystyle(1+C_{1})\kappa\leq\frac{7}{16},
that follows from the CFL condition (
6.3
). Thus, we obtain
\tilde{\eta}_{j+\frac{1}{2}}^{n}\geq 0,
for all
{j\in\mathbb{Z}}.

Now, collecting the estimates (
6.25
), (
6.47
), (
6.48
) and (
6.54
), we deduce that

\displaystyle\tilde{\theta}_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{4}{3}\gamma_{1}-8\gamma_{2}(\lvert\beta\rvert+\kappa)-16\lvert\beta\rvert\gamma_{2}C_{0}-8\gamma_{2}\kappa C_{0}C_{1}\geq\gamma_{1}>0,\,\,\mbox{for all}\,\,{j\in\mathbb{Z}},

(6.59)

as the CFL condition (
6.3
) implies that
\displaystyle\kappa\leq\frac{\gamma_{1}}{24\gamma_{2}\left(2(1+C_{0})+C_{0}C_{1}\right)}
for
\Delta t\leq\Delta x.
We also obtain an upper bound on
\tilde{\theta}_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n},
by invoking (
6.25
), (
6.47
), (
6.48
) and (
6.54
), as follows

\displaystyle\tilde{\theta}_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}\leq C_{2},\,\,\mbox{where}\,\,C_{2}:=2\kappa^{2}+\frac{4}{3}\gamma_{2}+8\kappa\gamma_{2}(2+2C_{0}+C_{0}C_{1}).

(6.60)

Finally, in light of the equation (
6.44
), the expression (
6.17
) reduces to

\displaystyle

\displaystyle S(k_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}},u^{n+1}_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}})-\frac{1}{2}\left(S(k_{j},u^{n}_{j})+S(k_{j+1},u^{n}_{j+1})\right)+\lambda\left(G(k_{j+1},u^{n}_{j+1})-G(k_{j},u^{n}_{j}))\right)

(6.61)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{8}\tilde{\eta}_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}(\Delta u_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n})^{2}+\frac{1}{16}\tilde{\theta}_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}\lambda(\Delta u_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n})^{3}+\Phi_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}},

where
\Phi_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}=\mathcal{O}(\Delta k_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}})+\mathcal{O}(k_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}-k_{j})+\mathcal{O}(k_{j+1}-k_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}).

Summing (
6.61
) over
n=0,1,\dots,N-1
and
j+\frac{n}{2}\in\mathbb{Z}
with
\lvert j\rvert\leq J,
and with the choice
\displaystyle S(k,u)=\frac{u^{2}}{2}
yields

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\lvert j\rvert\leq J}(u^{N}_{j})^{2}-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\lvert j\rvert\leq J}(u^{0}_{j})^{2}+\lambda\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\left(G(k_{J},u^{n}_{J})-G(k_{-J},u^{n}_{-J})\right)

(6.62)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{8}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lvert j\rvert\leq J\\
j+\frac{n}{2}\in\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}\tilde{\eta}_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}(\Delta u_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n})^{2}+\frac{1}{16}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lvert j\rvert\leq J\\
j+\frac{n}{2}\in\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}\tilde{\theta}_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}\lambda(\Delta u_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n})^{3}_{-}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+\frac{1}{16}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lvert j\rvert\leq J\\
j+\frac{n}{2}\in\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}\tilde{\theta}_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}\lambda(\Delta u_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n})^{3}_{+}+\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lvert j\rvert\leq J\\
j+\frac{n}{2}\in\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}\Phi_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}.

Rearranging (
6.62
) and taking into account
\tilde{\eta}_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}\geq 0
(see (
6.58
)), it follows that

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{16}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lvert j\rvert\leq J\\
j+\frac{n}{2}\in\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}\tilde{\theta}_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}\lambda(\Delta u_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n})^{3}_{-}

(6.63)

\displaystyle{\hskip 14.22636pt}\leq\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\lvert j\rvert\leq J}(u^{0}_{j})^{2}-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\lvert j\rvert\leq J}(u^{N}_{j})^{2}-\lambda\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\left(G(k_{J},u^{n}_{J})-G(k_{-J},u^{n}_{-J})\right)

\displaystyle{\hskip 14.22636pt}\hskip 14.22636pt+\frac{1}{16}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lvert j\rvert\leq J\\
j+\frac{n}{2}\in\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}\tilde{\theta}_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}\lambda(\Delta u_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n})^{3}_{+}+C_{3}N\lVert k\rVert_{BV}.

for some constant
C_{3}\geq 0.

Now, summing (
6.2
) of Lemma
9
over
n=0,\dots,N-1,
we see that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{500}\lambda\gamma_{1}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lvert j\rvert\leq J\\
j+\frac{n}{2}\in\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}(\Delta u_{{j-\frac{1}{2}}}^{n})_{+}^{3}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{500}\lambda\gamma_{1}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\sum_{j+\frac{n}{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}(\Delta u_{{j-\frac{1}{2}}}^{n})_{+}^{3}

(6.64)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\sum_{j+\frac{n}{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}\left((\Delta u^{n}_{{j-\frac{1}{2}}})_{+}^{2}-\left(\Delta u^{n+1}_{j}\right)_{+}^{2}\right)+\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\Psi\lVert k\rVert_{BV}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{{j\in\mathbb{Z}}}(\Delta u^{0}_{{j-\frac{1}{2}}})_{+}^{2}-\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\left(\Delta u^{N}_{j}\right)_{+}^{2}+\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\Psi\lVert k\rVert_{BV}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}(\Delta u^{0}_{{j-\frac{1}{2}}})_{+}^{2}+\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\Psi\lVert k\rVert_{BV},

\displaystyle\leq 2\lVert u_{0}\rVert\lVert u_{0}\rVert_{BV}+\Psi\lVert k\rVert_{BV}N.

The estimate (
6.64
) derived above, together with the bound
\gamma_{1}<\tilde{\theta}_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}\leq C_{2}
given in (
6.59
) and (
6.60
), applied to (
6.63
), yields

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{16}\gamma_{1}\lambda\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lvert j\rvert\leq J\\
j+\frac{n}{2}\in\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}(\Delta u_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n})^{3}_{-}

(6.65)

\displaystyle{\hskip 14.22636pt}\leq\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\lvert j\rvert\leq J}(u^{0}_{j})^{2}-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\lvert j\rvert\leq J}(u^{N}_{j})^{2}-\lambda\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\left(G(k_{J},u^{n}_{J})-G(k_{-J},u^{n}_{-J})\right)

\displaystyle{\hskip 14.22636pt}\hskip 14.22636pt+\frac{125}{4}\frac{C_{2}}{\gamma_{1}}\left(2\lVert u_{0}\rVert\lVert u_{0}\rVert_{BV}\right)+\left(\frac{125}{4}\frac{C_{2}}{\gamma_{1}}\Psi N+C_{3}N\right)\lVert k\rVert_{BV}

\displaystyle{\hskip 14.22636pt}\leq\frac{1}{2}\lVert u_{0}\rVert^{2}(2J+1)+2\lambda\lVert G\rVert N+\frac{125}{4}\frac{C_{2}}{\gamma_{1}}\left(2\lVert u_{0}\rVert\lVert u_{0}\rVert_{BV}\right)

\displaystyle{\hskip 14.22636pt}{\hskip 14.22636pt}+\left(\frac{125}{4}\frac{C_{2}}{\gamma_{1}}\Psi N+C_{3}N\right)\lVert k\rVert_{BV}.

Observe that

\displaystyle\Delta x\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lvert j\rvert\leq J\\
j+\frac{n}{2}\in\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}\lvert\Delta u_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}\rvert^{3}=\Delta x\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lvert j\rvert\leq J\\
j+\frac{n}{2}\in\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}\left((\Delta u_{{j-\frac{1}{2}}}^{n})_{+}^{3}-(\Delta u_{{j-\frac{1}{2}}}^{n})_{-}^{3}\right).

(6.66)

Now, using the estimates (
6.64
) and (
6.65
) in the above equation, we obtain the desired estimate (
6.4
)
with

\displaystyle C(X,T)

\displaystyle:=\frac{C_{4}}{\lambda\gamma_{1}}1000\left(1+\frac{C_{2}}{\gamma_{1}}\right)\lVert u_{0}\rVert\lVert u_{0}\rVert_{BV}+\frac{32}{\lambda\gamma_{1}}\lVert u_{0}\rVert^{2}X

\displaystyle{\hskip 14.22636pt}\hskip 14.22636pt+\frac{32}{\lambda\gamma_{1}}\lVert G\rVert T+\frac{1}{\lambda^{2}\gamma_{1}}T\left(500\Psi+500\frac{C_{2}}{\gamma_{1}}\Psi+16C_{3}\right)\lVert k\rVert_{BV},

when
\Delta x\leq C_{4},
for a constant
C_{4}>0.
This concludes the proof.

Now, we derive a quadratic estimate on the spatial differences of the approximate solutions.

Lemma 11
.

(Quadratic estimate)
Let the initial datum
u_{0}\in(\mathrm{L}^{\infty}\cap\mathrm{BV})(\mathbb{R})
be such that
\lVert u_{0}\rVert\leq C_{u_{0}}.
Consider the cell-average approximate solutions
\{u^{n}_{j}\}
generated by the scheme (
3.4
) and define

\displaystyle\nu_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{8}(1-4(\beta^{n}_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}})^{2})\left[1-\frac{1}{16}(1-4(\beta^{n}_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}})^{2})\left(\frac{\Delta\sigma_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}}{\Delta u_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}}\right)^{2}\right.

(6.67)

\displaystyle\left.{\hskip 14.22636pt}-\beta^{n}_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}\left(\frac{\Delta\sigma_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}}{\Delta u_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}}\right)-\frac{\sigma_{j+1}^{n}+\sigma_{j}^{n}}{2\Delta u_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}}\right]f_{uu}(k_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}},\tilde{u}_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}),

with
\beta_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}:=\lambda\tilde{a}_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n},
where
\tilde{a}_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}
and
\tilde{u}_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}
are as in (
6.26
). If the CFL condition (
6.3
) holds, then we have:

(i)
\nu_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}\geq 0
for
{j\in\mathbb{Z}},
and

(ii) For any fixed
T,X,N
and
J
as in Lemma
10
, the following estimate holds:

\displaystyle\Delta x\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\nu_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}(\Delta u_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n})^{2}\leq K(X,T),

(6.68)

where
K(X,T)
is a constant independent of
\Delta x.

Proof.

Recall the expression (
6.17
):

\displaystyle\bar{E}_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}

\displaystyle=I+\bar{J}+R_{1}^{n}+R_{2}^{n}+\tilde{R}_{1}^{n}+\tilde{R}_{2}^{n},

(6.69)

where
I
and
\bar{J}
is as in (
6.19
) and (
6.14
), respectively.
Choosing
S(k,u)=f(k,u)-f(k,c)
in (
6.19
) and using the fact that
\bar{g}_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{\prime}=\frac{\Delta g_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}}{\Delta u_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}},
we write

\displaystyle I

\displaystyle=-(\Delta u_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n})^{2}\left(\frac{1}{4}-\left(\lambda\frac{\Delta g_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}}{\Delta u_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}}\right)^{2}\right)\int_{0}^{1}\int_{0}^{1}sf_{uu}(k_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}},u_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}(r,s))\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}s\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}r.

Now, owing to hypothesis
(H2)
, we expand
f_{uu}
in the second variable about the point
(k_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}},\tilde{u}_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n})
using a Taylor series, and write the term
I
as

\displaystyle I

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{8}(\Delta u_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n})^{2}\left(1-\left(2\lambda\frac{\Delta g_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}}{\Delta u_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}}\right)^{2}\right)f_{uu}(k_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}},\tilde{u}_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n})+\mathcal{O}((\Delta u_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}})^{3})

(6.70)

\displaystyle{\hskip 14.22636pt}+\mathcal{O}(\Delta k_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}),

where
\tilde{u}_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}
is defined as in (
6.26
). The above expression uses the fact that

\displaystyle u_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}(r,s)-\tilde{u}_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\Delta u_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}\left(1-s(1+r)\right)-\lambda\frac{\Delta g_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}}{\Delta u_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}}s(1-r)\Delta u_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}(\Delta u_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n})+\mathcal{O}(\Delta k_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}).

Further, the choice
S(k,u)=f(k,u)-f(k,c)
and the trapezoidal rule of integration along with (
6.22
) simplify
\bar{J}
in (
6.14
) to

\displaystyle\bar{J}

\displaystyle=\lambda\int_{0}^{1}f_{uu}(k_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}},u(s))\left(f(k_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}},u(s))-\bar{g}_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}(u(s)\right)u^{\prime}(s)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle=-\frac{\lambda}{2}\Delta u_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}\left[f_{uu}(k_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}},u_{{j+1}}^{n})\left(f(k_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}},u_{{j+1}}^{n})-g(k_{{j+1}},u_{{j+1}}^{n})\right)\right.

\displaystyle{\hskip 14.22636pt}+\left.f_{uu}(k_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}},u_{j}^{n})\left(f(k_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}},u_{j}^{n})-g(k_{j},u_{j}^{n})\right)\right]+\mathcal{O}((\Delta u_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n})^{3})

\displaystyle=-\frac{\lambda}{2}\Delta u_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}\left[f_{uu}(k_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}},u_{{j+1}}^{n})\left(\frac{\lambda}{2}(a_{j+1}^{n})^{2}\sigma_{j+1}^{n}-\frac{1}{8}(\lambda a_{j+1}^{n}\sigma_{j+1}^{n})^{2}f_{uu}(k_{j+1},\zeta_{3})\right.\right.

\displaystyle{\hskip 14.22636pt}{\hskip 14.22636pt}\left.\left.-\frac{1}{8\lambda}\sigma_{j+1}^{n}\right)\right.

\displaystyle{\hskip 14.22636pt}+\left.f_{uu}(k_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}},u_{j}^{n})\left(\frac{\lambda}{2}(a_{j}^{n})^{2}\sigma_{j}^{n}-\frac{1}{8}(\lambda a_{j}^{n}\sigma_{j}^{n})^{2}f_{uu}(k_{j},\zeta_{2})-\frac{1}{8\lambda}\sigma_{j}^{n}\right)\right]

\displaystyle{\hskip 14.22636pt}+\mathcal{O}((\Delta u_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n})^{3})+\mathcal{O}(\Delta k_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}})+\mathcal{O}(k_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}-k_{j})+\mathcal{O}(k_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}-k_{j+1}).

Again, noting that
\sigma_{j}^{n},\sigma_{{j+1}}^{n}
are
\mathcal{O}(\Delta u_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}),
replacing
(a_{j}^{n})^{2}
and
(a_{j+1}^{n})^{2}
from their expression in (
6.28
), and subsequently expanding
f_{uu}
in a Taylor series about the point
(k_{j+\frac{1}{2}},\tilde{u}_{j+\frac{1}{2}}^{n}),
the term
\bar{J}
reduces to

\displaystyle\bar{J}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{16}\Delta u_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}(1-4(\beta_{j+\frac{1}{2}}^{n})^{2})\left[f_{uu}(k_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}},u_{{j+1}}^{n})\sigma_{j+1}^{n}+f_{uu}(k_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}},u_{j}^{n})\sigma_{j}^{n}\right]

(6.71)

\displaystyle{\hskip 14.22636pt}+\mathcal{O}((\Delta u_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n})^{3})+\mathcal{O}(\Delta k_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}})+\mathcal{O}(k_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}-k_{j})+\mathcal{O}(k_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}-k_{j+1})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{16}(\Delta u_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n})^{2}(1-4(\beta_{j+\frac{1}{2}}^{n})^{2})f_{uu}(k_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}},\tilde{u}_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n})\frac{(\sigma_{j+1}^{n}+\sigma_{j}^{n})}{\Delta u_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}}+\mathcal{O}((\Delta u_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n})^{3})

\displaystyle{\hskip 14.22636pt}+\mathcal{O}(\Delta k_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}})+\mathcal{O}(k_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}-k_{j})+\mathcal{O}(k_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}-k_{j+1}).

Now, we note that the term

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{8}(\Delta u_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n})^{2}\left(1-\left(2\lambda\frac{\Delta g_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}}{\Delta u_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}}\right)^{2}\right),

which appears in (
6.70
), is nothing but the value of
I
obtained with the choice
S(k,u)=\frac{u^{2}}{2},
see (
6.32
). Therefore, we replace this term in (
6.70
) with the right-hand side of (
6.40
). The resulting
I
of (
6.70
) is added with the term
\bar{J}
in (
6.71
), to yield

\displaystyle I+\bar{J}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{8}(1-4(\beta_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n})^{2})\left[1-\frac{1}{16}(1-4(\beta_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n})^{2})\left(\frac{\Delta\sigma_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}}{\Delta u_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}}\right)^{2}\right.

(6.72)

\displaystyle}{}{\displaystyle\left.-(\beta_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}+\mathcal{A}_{4})\left(\frac{\Delta\sigma_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}}{\Delta u_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}}\right)-\frac{(\sigma_{j}^{n}+\sigma_{j+1}^{n})}{2\Delta u_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}}\right]f_{uu}(k_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}},\tilde{u}_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n})(\Delta u_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n})^{2}

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle+\mathcal{O}((\Delta u_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n})^{3})+\mathcal{O}(\Delta k_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}})+\mathcal{O}(k_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}-k_{j})+\mathcal{O}(k_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}-k_{j+1}),

where
\mathcal{A}_{4}
is as in (
6.39
). Since
\mathcal{A}_{4}=\mathcal{O}(\Delta u_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n})
(see (
6.48
)), the term above turns into

\displaystyle I+\bar{J}

\displaystyle=-\nu_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}(\Delta u_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n})^{2}+\mathcal{O}((\Delta u_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n})^{3})+\mathcal{O}(\Delta k_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}),

(6.73)

where
\nu_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}
is given by (
6.67
).
Now, to show that
\nu_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}\geq 0,
first we first observe that
1-4(\beta_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n})^{2}\geq 0,
by the CFL condition (
6.3
) and
f_{uu}(k_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}},\tilde{u}_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n})\geq 0,
by hypothesis
(H2)
. This, together with an argument similar to (
6.58
) now implies that
\nu_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}\geq 0.
Finally, observing that
R_{1}^{n}+R_{2}^{n}+\tilde{R}_{1}^{n}+\tilde{R}_{2}^{n}=\mathcal{O}(k_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}-k_{j})+\mathcal{O}(k_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}-k_{j+1})
(see (
6.7
) and (
6.16
)) and calling the expression (
6.73
) in (
6.17
), we write

\displaystyle\bar{E}_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}

\displaystyle=-\nu_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}(\Delta u_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n})^{2}+\mathcal{O}((\Delta u_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n})^{3})+\mathcal{O}(\Delta k_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}})

(6.74)

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle+\mathcal{O}(k_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}-k_{j})+\mathcal{O}(k_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}-k_{j+1}).

Recalling the notation (see (
6.18
)),

\displaystyle\bar{E}_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}

\displaystyle:=S(k_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}},u^{n+1}_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}})-\frac{1}{2}\left(S(k_{j},u_{j}^{n})+S(k_{j+1},u_{j+1}^{n})\right)

\displaystyle{\hskip 14.22636pt}+\lambda\left(G(k_{j+1},u_{j+1}^{n})-G(k_{j},u_{j}^{n})\right),

summing (
6.74
) over
n=0,1,\dots,N-1,

j+\frac{n}{2}\in\mathbb{Z}
with
\lvert j\rvert\leq J,
and multiplying by
\Delta x,
we obtain

\displaystyle\Delta x\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lvert j\rvert\leq J\\
j+\frac{n}{2}\in\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}\nu_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}(\Delta u_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n})^{2}

(6.75)

\displaystyle\leq\Delta x\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lvert j\rvert\leq J\\
j+\frac{n}{2}\in\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{2}\left(S(k_{j},u_{j}^{0})+S(k_{j+1},u_{j+1}^{0})\right)-\Delta x\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lvert j\rvert\leq J\\
j+\frac{n}{2}\in\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}S(k_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}},u^{N}_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}})

\displaystyle{\hskip 14.22636pt}+\Delta t\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}G(k_{-J},u_{-J}^{n})-\Delta t\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}G(k_{J},u_{J}^{n})

\displaystyle{\hskip 14.22636pt}+K_{1}\Delta x\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lvert j\rvert\leq J\\
j+\frac{n}{2}\in\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}\lvert\Delta u_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}\rvert^{3}+K_{2}N\Delta x\lVert k\rVert_{BV},

for some constants
K_{1},K_{2}\geq 0.
Finally, since
\lVert S\rVert\leq 2\lVert f\rVert
for the choice
S(k,u)=f(k,u)-f(k,c),
using the cubic estimate (
6.4
) from Lemma
10
and the boundedness of
G
in (
6.75
), we obtain the desired estimate (
11
), with

K(X,T):=8X\lVert f\rVert+2\lVert G\rVert T+K_{1}C(X,T)+\frac{1}{\lambda}K_{2}\lVert k\rVert_{BV}T,

and
C(X,T)
is as in (
6.4
), thus completing the proof.

7
Convergence of the second-order scheme

As a first step in proving convergence to a weak solution, we provide the
\mathrm{W}^{-1,2}_{\mathrm{loc}}
compactness in the following lemma, which is necessary for applying the compensated compactness result in Theorem
6
.

Lemma 12
(
\mathrm{W}^{-1,2}_{\mathrm{loc}}\mbox{compactness}
).

Let
u_{0}\in(\mathrm{L}^{\infty}\cap\mathrm{BV})(\mathbb{R}).
Under the CFL condition (
6.3
), for the approximate solutions
u_{\Delta}
in (
3.8
) and the pairs
(S_{i},Q_{i})
considered in (
4.1
), the sequence of distributions

\left\{S_{i}(k(x),u_{\Delta})_{t}+Q_{i}(k(x),u_{\Delta})_{x}\right\}_{\Delta>0},

is contained in a compact subset of
\mathrm{W}^{-1,2}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{+})
, for
i=1,2.

Proof.

Suppressing the index of
(S_{i},Q_{i}),
we denote

\mathcal{L}^{\Delta}:=-(S(u^{\Delta})_{t}+Q(k(x),u^{\Delta})_{x})

which is defined by the action

\displaystyle\left\langle\mathcal{L}^{\Delta},\phi\right\rangle

\displaystyle:=\int_{\mathbb{R_{+}}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(S(k(x),u_{\Delta})\phi_{t}+Q(k(x),u_{\Delta})\phi_{x}\right)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t,

(7.1)

for
\phi\in W^{1,2}_{0,\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}).

Using
k_{\Delta}
as defined in (
3.8
), we add and subtract
S(k_{\Delta}(x),u_{\Delta})
and
Q(k_{\Delta}(x),u_{\Delta})
in the integrand of (
7.1
), which in turn allows us to write
\mathcal{L}^{\Delta}=\hat{\mathcal{L}}^{\Delta}+\tilde{\mathcal{L}}^{\Delta},
where

\displaystyle\langle\hat{\mathcal{L}}^{\Delta},\phi\rangle

\displaystyle:=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{+}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(S(k(x),u_{\Delta})-S(k_{\Delta}(x),u_{\Delta})\right)\phi_{t}\,\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\,\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle{\hskip 14.22636pt}+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{+}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(Q(k(x),u_{\Delta})-Q(k_{\Delta}(x),u_{\Delta})\right)\phi_{x}\,\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\,\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t,

\displaystyle\langle\tilde{\mathcal{L}}^{\Delta},\phi\rangle

\displaystyle:=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{+}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(S(k_{\Delta}(x),u_{\Delta})\phi_{t}+Q(k_{\Delta}(x),u_{\Delta})\phi_{x}\right)\,\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\,\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t.

Now, we consider a bounded open subset
\Omega
of
\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}
and let
X>0,

T>0
be such that
\Omega\subseteq[-X,X]\times[0,T].
Further, choose smallest integers
J,N\in\mathbb{N}
such that
J\Delta x>X+\Delta x,
and
N\Delta t>T.
Additionally, define
S_{j}^{n}:=S(k_{\Delta}(x_{j},t^{n}),u_{\Delta}(x_{j},t^{n}))=S(k_{j}^{n},u_{j}^{n}),

Q_{j}^{n}:=Q(k_{\Delta}(x_{j},t^{n}),u_{\Delta}(x_{j},t^{n}))=Q(k_{j}^{n},u_{j}^{n}).

Let
q\in(1,2)
and
\displaystyle p=\frac{q}{q-1}.
Now, applying Hölder’s inequality to the term
\langle\hat{\mathcal{L}}^{\Delta},\phi\rangle
with
\phi\in W_{0}^{1,p}(\Omega),
we obtain

\displaystyle\lim_{\Delta\rightarrow 0}\lvert\langle\hat{\mathcal{L}}^{\Delta},\phi\rangle\rvert\leq\lim_{\Delta\rightarrow 0}\left(\lVert\partial_{k}S\rVert+\lVert\partial_{k}Q\rVert\right)\lVert k-k_{\Delta}\rVert_{L^{q}(\Omega)}\lVert\phi\rVert_{W_{0}^{1,p}(\Omega)}=0,

from which we can conclude that

\displaystyle\{\hat{\mathcal{L}}^{\Delta}\}\,\mbox{is compact in}\,\mathrm{W}^{-1,q}(\Omega)\,\,\mbox{for any}\,q\in(1,2).

(7.2)

Next, we focus on showing that
\{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}^{\Delta}\}_{\Delta>0}\,\mbox{is compact in}\,\mathrm{W}^{-1,q}(\Omega)\,\mbox{for some}\,q\in(1,2).
We begin with applying summation by parts to expand the term
\left\langle\tilde{\mathcal{L}}^{\Delta},\phi\right\rangle
as

\displaystyle\left\langle\tilde{\mathcal{L}}^{\Delta},\phi\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lvert j\rvert\leq J\\
j+\frac{n}{2}\in\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}\int_{t^{n}}^{t^{n+1}}\int_{x_{j-\frac{1}{2}}}^{x_{j+\frac{1}{2}}}\left(S(k_{\Delta}(x),u_{\Delta})\phi_{t}+Q(k_{\Delta}(x),u_{\Delta})\phi_{x}\right)\,\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\,\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t.

\displaystyle=\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lvert j\rvert\leq J\\
j+\frac{n}{2}\in\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}\Bigg[\int_{x_{j-\frac{1}{2}}}^{x_{j+\frac{1}{2}}}S_{j}^{n}\left(\phi(x,t_{n+1})-\phi(x,t_{n})\right)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle{\hskip 14.22636pt}+\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}Q_{j}^{n}\left(\phi(x_{j+\frac{1}{2}},t)-\phi(x_{j-\frac{1}{2}},t)\right)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t\Bigg]

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lvert j\rvert\leq J\\
j+\frac{n}{2}\in\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}\int_{x_{j-\frac{1}{2}}}^{x_{j+\frac{1}{2}}}S_{j}^{N-1}\phi(x,N\Delta t)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lvert j\rvert\leq J\\
j+\frac{n}{2}\in\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}\int_{x_{j-\frac{1}{2}}}^{x_{j+\frac{1}{2}}}S_{j}^{0}\phi(x,0)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle{\hskip 14.22636pt}-\sum_{n=1}^{N-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lvert j\rvert\leq J\\
j+\frac{n}{2}\in\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}\Bigg[\int_{x_{j-\frac{1}{2}}}^{x_{j}}\left(S_{j}^{n}-S_{j-\frac{1}{2}}^{n-1}\right)\phi(x,t_{n})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle{\hskip 14.22636pt}{\hskip 14.22636pt}+\int_{x_{j}}^{x_{j+\frac{1}{2}}}\left(S_{j}^{n}-S_{j+\frac{1}{2}}^{n-1}\right)\phi(x,t_{n})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\Bigg]

\displaystyle{\hskip 14.22636pt}-\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lvert j\rvert\leq J\\
j+\frac{n}{2}\in\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}\int_{t^{n}}^{t_{n+1}}\left(Q_{j+1}^{n}-Q_{j}^{n}\right)\phi(x_{j+\frac{1}{2}},t)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t.

Further, denoting
\phi_{j}^{n}:=\phi(x_{j},t^{n})
and adding and subtracting suitable terms, we write

\displaystyle\left\langle\tilde{\mathcal{L}}^{\Delta},\phi\right\rangle

\displaystyle=:<\tilde{\mathcal{L}}^{\Delta}_{0},\phi>+<\tilde{\mathcal{L}}^{\Delta}_{1},\phi>+<\tilde{\mathcal{L}}^{\Delta}_{2},\phi>+<\tilde{\mathcal{L}}^{\Delta}_{3},\phi>+<\tilde{\mathcal{L}}^{\Delta}_{4},\phi>,

(7.3)

where

\displaystyle<\tilde{\mathcal{L}}^{\Delta}_{0},\phi>

\displaystyle:=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lvert j\rvert\leq J\\
j+\frac{n}{2}\in\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}\int_{x_{j-\frac{1}{2}}}^{x_{j+\frac{1}{2}}}S_{j}^{N-1}\phi(x,N\Delta t)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lvert j\rvert\leq J\\
j+\frac{n}{2}\in\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}\int_{x_{j-\frac{1}{2}}}^{x_{j+\frac{1}{2}}}S_{j}^{0}\phi(x,0)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x,

(7.4)

\displaystyle<\tilde{\mathcal{L}}^{\Delta}_{1},\phi>

\displaystyle:=\sum_{n=1}^{N-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lvert j\rvert\leq J\\
j+\frac{n}{2}\in\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}\int_{x_{j-\frac{1}{2}}}^{x_{j}}\left(S_{j}^{n}-S_{j-\frac{1}{2}}^{n-1}\right)\left(\phi_{j}^{n}-\phi(x,t_{n})\right)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x,

\displaystyle<\tilde{\mathcal{L}}^{\Delta}_{2},\phi>

\displaystyle:=\sum_{n=1}^{N-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lvert j\rvert\leq J\\
j+\frac{n}{2}\in\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}\int_{x_{j}}^{x_{j+\frac{1}{2}}}\left(S_{j}^{n}-S_{j+\frac{1}{2}}^{n-1}\right)\left(\phi_{j}^{n}-\phi(x,t_{n})\right)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x,

\displaystyle<\tilde{\mathcal{L}}^{\Delta}_{3},\phi>

\displaystyle:=\sum_{n=1}^{N}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lvert j\rvert\leq J\\
j+\frac{n-1}{2}\in\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}\int_{t_{n-1}}^{t_{n}}\left(Q_{j+\frac{1}{2}}^{n-1}-Q_{j-\frac{1}{2}}^{n-1}\right)\left(\phi_{j}^{n}-\phi(x_{j},t)\right)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t,

\displaystyle<\tilde{\mathcal{L}}^{\Delta}_{4},\phi>

\displaystyle:=-\Delta x\sum_{n=1}^{N-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lvert j\rvert\leq J\\
j+\frac{n}{2}\in\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}\phi_{j}^{n}\left(S_{j}^{n}-\frac{1}{2}\left(S_{j+\frac{1}{2}}^{n-1}+S_{j-\frac{1}{2}}^{n-1}\right)+\lambda\left(Q_{j+\frac{1}{2}}^{n-1}-Q_{j-\frac{1}{2}}^{n-1}\right)\right).

Hereafter, we set
S(k,u)=S_{2}(k,u)=f(k,u)-f(k,c),\,\,Q(k,u)=Q_{2}(k,u)=\int_{c}^{u}(f_{u}(k,\xi))^{2}\,\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi.
The proof for the case
S_{1}(u)=u-c,\,\,Q_{1}(k,u)=f(k,u)-f(k,c)
follows analogously and is omitted.
Now, using a change of index, we write the term
<\tilde{\mathcal{L}}^{\Delta}_{4},\phi>
in (
7.3
) as

\displaystyle<\tilde{\mathcal{L}}^{\Delta}_{4},\phi>

\displaystyle=\Delta x\sum_{n=0}^{N-2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lvert j\rvert\leq J\\
j+\frac{n+1}{2}\in\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}\phi_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n+1}\left[S_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n+1}-\frac{1}{2}\left(S_{j+1}^{n}+S_{j}^{n}\right)+\lambda\left(Q_{j+1}^{n}-Q_{j}^{n}\right)\right].

(7.5)

Proceeding as in the proof of Lemma
10
(see (
6.5
)-(
6.19
)) and using the same notations, we can write

\displaystyle S_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n+1}-\frac{1}{2}\left(S_{j+1}^{n}+S_{j}^{n}\right)+\lambda\left(Q_{j+1}^{n}-Q_{j}^{n}\right)

(7.6)

\displaystyle=I+\bar{J}-\lambda\Bigl(S_{u}(k_{j},u_{j}^{n})(f(k_{j},u_{j}^{n})-g(k_{j},u_{j}^{n}))

\displaystyle{\hskip 14.22636pt}-S_{u}(k_{j+1},u_{j+1}^{n})(f(k_{j+1},u_{j+1}^{n})-g(k_{j+1},u_{j+1}^{n}))\Bigr)+\tilde{R}_{1}+\tilde{R}_{2}+R_{1}+R_{2}.

Now, invoking (
7.6
) in (
7.5
), we write

\displaystyle<\tilde{\mathcal{L}}_{4}^{\Delta},\phi>

\displaystyle=<\tilde{\mathcal{L}}_{4,1}^{\Delta},\phi>+<\tilde{\mathcal{L}}_{4,2}^{\Delta},\phi>+<\tilde{\mathcal{L}}^{\Delta}_{4,3},\phi>,

(7.7)

where we define

\displaystyle<\tilde{\mathcal{L}}^{\Delta}_{4,1},\phi>

\displaystyle:=\sum_{n=0}^{N-2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lvert j\rvert\leq J\\
j+\frac{n+1}{2}\in\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}\Delta x\phi_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n+1}\left(I+\bar{J}\right),

(7.8)

\displaystyle<\tilde{\mathcal{L}}^{\Delta}_{4,2},\phi>

\displaystyle:=-\sum_{n=0}^{N-2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lvert j\rvert\leq J\\
j+\frac{n+1}{2}\in\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}\Delta x\phi_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n+1}\lambda\Bigl(S_{u}(k_{j},u_{j}^{n})(f(k_{j},u_{j}^{n})-g(k_{j},u_{j}^{n}))

\displaystyle{\hskip 14.22636pt}{\hskip 14.22636pt}-S_{u}(k_{j+1},u_{j+1}^{n})(f(k_{j+1},u_{j+1}^{n})-g(k_{j+1},u_{j+1}^{n}))\Bigr),

\displaystyle<\tilde{\mathcal{L}}^{\Delta}_{4,3},\phi>

\displaystyle:=\sum_{n=0}^{N-2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lvert j\rvert\leq J\\
j+\frac{n+1}{2}\in\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}\Delta x\phi_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n+1}\left(\tilde{R}_{1}+\tilde{R}_{2}+R_{1}+R_{2}\right).

At this stage, by summarizing (
7.3
) and (
7.8
), we write

\displaystyle\left\langle\tilde{\mathcal{L}}^{\Delta},\phi\right\rangle

\displaystyle=:<\tilde{\mathcal{L}}^{\Delta}_{0},\phi>+<\tilde{\mathcal{L}}^{\Delta}_{1},\phi>+<\tilde{\mathcal{L}}^{\Delta}_{2},\phi>+<\tilde{\mathcal{L}}^{\Delta}_{3},\phi>

(7.9)

\displaystyle{\hskip 14.22636pt}+<\tilde{\mathcal{L}}_{4,1}^{\Delta},\phi>+<\tilde{\mathcal{L}}_{4,2}^{\Delta},\phi>+<\tilde{\mathcal{L}}^{\Delta}_{4,3},\phi>,

and proceed with the proof of compactness of
\tilde{\mathcal{L}}^{\Delta}
in two steps.

Step 1
(Compactness of
\tilde{\mathcal{L}}^{\Delta}_{4,1},\tilde{\mathcal{L}}^{\Delta}_{4,3}\,\,\mbox{and}\,\,\tilde{\mathcal{L}}^{\Delta}_{0}
):
Using (
6.73
) from Lemma
11
, we write

\displaystyle

\displaystyle<\tilde{\mathcal{L}}^{\Delta}_{4,1},\phi>

(7.10)

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle=\sum_{n=0}^{N-2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lvert j\rvert\leq J\\
j+\frac{n+1}{2}\in\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}\Delta x\phi_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n+1}\left(-\nu_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}(\Delta u_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n})^{2}+\mathcal{O}((\Delta u_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n})^{3})+\mathcal{O}(\Delta k_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}})\right).

Again, the cubic estimate (
6.4
) from Lemma
10
and the quadratic estimate (
6.68
) from Lemma
11
, together with the hypothesis
(H1)
yield

\displaystyle\lvert<\tilde{\mathcal{L}}^{\Delta}_{4,1},\phi>\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\Delta x\lVert\phi\rVert\sum_{n=0}^{N-2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lvert j\rvert\leq J\\
j+\frac{n+1}{2}\in\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}\nu_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}(\Delta u_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n})^{2}

(7.11)

\displaystyle{\hskip 14.22636pt}+\mathcal{K}_{1}\Delta x\lVert\phi\rVert\sum_{n=0}^{N-2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lvert j\rvert\leq J\\
j+\frac{n+1}{2}\in\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}\lvert\Delta u_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}\rvert^{3}+\mathcal{K}_{2}\Delta x\lVert\phi\rVert\sum_{n=0}^{N-2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lvert j\rvert\leq J\\
j+\frac{n+1}{2}\in\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}\lvert\Delta k_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{K}_{3}\lVert\phi\rVert,\quad\mbox{for}\quad\phi\in C_{0}(\Omega),

and some constants
\mathcal{K}_{1},\mathcal{K}_{2}>0
and
\displaystyle K_{3}:=K(X,T)+\mathcal{K}_{1}C(X,T)+\frac{1}{\lambda}\mathcal{K}_{2}\lVert k\rVert_{BV}T.
Here,
C_{0}(\Omega)
denotes the space of continuous functions on
\Omega
that vanish at the boundary.

In the next step, we estimate
<\tilde{\mathcal{L}}^{\Delta}_{4,3},\phi>

for
\phi\in C_{0}(\Omega)
by recalling (
6.7
), (
6.16
) and using hypothesis
(H1)
, as

\displaystyle\lvert<\tilde{\mathcal{L}}^{\Delta}_{4,3},\phi>\rvert\leq\lVert\phi\rVert\mathcal{K}_{4}\sum_{n=0}^{N-2}\Delta x\lVert k\rVert_{\mathrm{BV}}\leq\mathcal{K}_{5}\lVert\phi\rVert,

(7.12)

where
\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{5}:=\lVert k\rVert_{BV}\frac{\mathcal{K}_{4}}{\lambda}T
and
\mathcal{K}_{4}>0
is some constant. Combining the bounds (
7.11
) and (
7.12
), we obtain the following estimate

\displaystyle\lVert\tilde{\mathcal{L}}^{\Delta}_{4,1}\rVert_{\mathcal{M}(\Omega)},\lVert\tilde{\mathcal{L}}^{\Delta}_{4,3}\rVert_{\mathcal{M}(\Omega)}\leq\mathcal{K}_{6}=\max\{\mathcal{K}_{3},\mathcal{K}_{5}\},

(7.13)

as
\mathcal{M}(\Omega),
the space of all bounded Radon measures on
\Omega,
is the dual of the space
\left(C_{0}(\Omega),\lVert\cdot\rVert_{\infty}\right)
( see
[
44
]
for more details). Similarly, dealing with the term
<\tilde{\mathcal{L}}^{\Delta}_{0},\phi>,
we obtain a bound

\displaystyle\lVert\tilde{\mathcal{L}}^{\Delta}_{0}\rVert_{\mathcal{M}(\Omega)}\leq\mathcal{K}_{7},

(7.14)

\mbox{as}\,\lvert<\tilde{\mathcal{L}}^{\Delta}_{0},\phi>\rvert\leq\mathcal{K}_{7}\lVert\phi\rVert
for some constant
\mathcal{K}_{7}>0.

By Sobolev’s imbedding theorem (see Lemma 2.55, page 38 in
[
44
]
), we have the inclusion
\mathcal{M}(\Omega)\subset\mathrm{W}^{-1,q}(\Omega)
with compact imbedding for
q\in[1,2).
Consequently, from (
7.13
) and (
7.14
), it follows that the sets

\displaystyle\{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}^{\Delta}_{4,1}\}_{\Delta>0},\{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}^{\Delta}_{4,3}\}_{\Delta>0}\,\,\mbox{and}\,\,\{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}^{\Delta}_{0}\}_{\Delta>0}\,\mbox{are compact in}\,\mathrm{W}^{-1,q}(\Omega),\,\forall\,q\in[1,2).

(7.15)

Step 2
(Compactness of
\tilde{\mathcal{L}}^{\Delta}_{1},\tilde{\mathcal{L}}^{\Delta}_{2},\tilde{\mathcal{L}}^{\Delta}_{3}\,\mbox{and}\,\tilde{\mathcal{L}}^{\Delta}_{4,2}
):
We first derive some estimates useful in bounding the term
<\tilde{\mathcal{L}}^{\Delta}_{1},\phi>
in (
7.3
).
Observing that

\displaystyle\Big\lvert u^{n-\frac{1}{2}}_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}-u^{n-\frac{1}{2}}_{{j-\frac{1}{2}}}\Big\rvert

\displaystyle=\Big\lvert\Delta u_{j}^{n-1}-\frac{\lambda}{2}f_{u}(k_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}},u^{n-1}_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}})\sigma_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n-1}+\frac{\lambda}{2}f_{u}(k_{{j-\frac{1}{2}}},u^{n-1}_{{j-\frac{1}{2}}})\sigma_{{j-\frac{1}{2}}}^{n-1}\Big\rvert

(7.16)

\displaystyle\leq\lvert\Delta u_{j}^{n-1}\rvert\left(1+\kappa\right),

we derive the following bound

\displaystyle\lvert u_{j}^{n}-u_{{j-\frac{1}{2}}}^{n-1}\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\Big\lvert\frac{1}{2}(u^{n-1}_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}-u^{n-1}_{{j-\frac{1}{2}}})-\frac{1}{8}(\sigma_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n-1}-\sigma_{{j-\frac{1}{2}}}^{n-1})-\lambda\left(f(k_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}},u^{n-1/2}_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}})-f(k_{{j-\frac{1}{2}}},u^{n-1/2}_{{j-\frac{1}{2}}})\right)\Big\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\Big\lvert u^{n-1}_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}-u^{n-1}_{{j-\frac{1}{2}}}\Big\rvert+\frac{1}{8}\left(\lvert\sigma_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n-1}\rvert+\lvert\sigma_{{j-\frac{1}{2}}}^{n-1}\rvert\right)

\displaystyle{\hskip 14.22636pt}+\Big\lvert\lambda\left(f(k_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}},u^{n-1/2}_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}})-f(k_{{j-\frac{1}{2}}},u^{n-1/2}_{{j-\frac{1}{2}}})\right)\Big\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{4}\right)\Big\lvert\Delta u_{j}^{n-1}\Big\rvert+\lambda\lVert f_{k}\rVert\lvert\Delta k_{j}\rvert+\kappa\Big\lvert u^{n-\frac{1}{2}}_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}-u^{n-\frac{1}{2}}_{{j-\frac{1}{2}}}\Big\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\lvert\Delta u_{j}^{n-1}\rvert\left(\frac{3}{4}+\kappa(1+\kappa)\right)+\lambda\lVert f_{k}\rVert\lvert\Delta k_{j}\rvert.

Again, using the above estimate, we obtain

\displaystyle\lvert S_{j}^{n}-S_{j-\frac{1}{2}}^{n-1}\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\lVert S_{k}\rVert\lvert k_{j}-k_{{j-\frac{1}{2}}}\rvert+\lVert S_{u}\rVert\lvert u_{j}^{n}-u_{{j-\frac{1}{2}}}^{n-1}\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\lVert S_{k}\rVert\lvert k_{j}-k_{{j-\frac{1}{2}}}\rvert+\mathcal{K}_{8}\lVert S_{u}\rVert\lvert\Delta u_{j}^{n-1}\rvert+\lVert S_{u}\rVert\lambda\lVert f_{k}\rVert\lvert\Delta k_{j}\rvert,

where
\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{8}:=\frac{3}{4}+\kappa(1+\kappa).
Using this estimate in
<\tilde{\mathcal{L}}^{\Delta}_{1},\phi>
and subsequently applying the Hölder’s inequality, we write

\displaystyle\lvert<\tilde{\mathcal{L}}^{\Delta}_{1},\phi>\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{n=1}^{N-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lvert j\rvert\leq J\\
j+\frac{n}{2}\in\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}\mathcal{K}_{8}\lVert S_{u}\rVert\lvert\Delta u_{j}^{n-1}\rvert\int_{x_{j-\frac{1}{2}}}^{x_{j}}\lvert\phi_{j}^{n}-\phi(x,t_{n})\rvert\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle{\hskip 14.22636pt}+\sum_{n=1}^{N-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lvert j\rvert\leq J\\
j+\frac{n}{2}\in\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}\left(\lVert S_{k}\rVert\lvert k_{j}-k_{{j-\frac{1}{2}}}\rvert+\lVert S_{u}\rVert\lambda\lVert f_{k}\rVert\lvert\Delta k_{j}\rvert\right)\int_{x_{j-\frac{1}{2}}}^{x_{j}}\lvert\phi_{j}^{n}-\phi(x,t_{n})\rvert\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{K}_{8}\sum_{n=1}^{N-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lvert j\rvert\leq J\\
j+\frac{n}{2}\in\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}\lVert S_{u}\rVert\lvert\Delta u_{j}^{n-1}\rvert\lVert\phi\rVert_{C_{0}^{\alpha}}\Delta x^{\alpha+1}

\displaystyle{\hskip 14.22636pt}+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{n=1}^{N-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lvert j\rvert\leq J\\
j+\frac{n}{2}\in\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}\left(\lVert S_{k}\rVert\lvert k_{j}-k_{{j-\frac{1}{2}}}\rvert+\lVert S_{u}\rVert\lambda\lVert f_{k}\rVert\lvert\Delta k_{j}\rvert\right)\lVert\phi\rVert_{C_{0}^{\alpha}}\Delta x^{\alpha+1}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{K}_{8}\lVert S_{u}\rVert\lVert\phi\rVert_{C_{0}^{\alpha}}\Delta x^{\alpha+\frac{2}{3}}\left(\Delta x\sum_{n=1}^{N-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lvert j\rvert\leq J\\
j+\frac{n}{2}\in\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}\lvert\Delta u_{j}^{n-1}\rvert^{3}\right)^{\frac{1}{3}}\left(\sum_{n=1}^{N-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lvert j\rvert\leq J\\
j+\frac{n}{2}\in\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}1\right)^{\frac{2}{3}}

\displaystyle{\hskip 14.22636pt}+\frac{1}{2\lambda}\left(\lVert S_{k}\rVert T+\lVert S_{u}\rVert\lambda\lVert f_{k}\rVert T\right)\lVert k\rVert_{BV}\lVert\phi\rVert_{C_{0}^{\alpha}}\Delta x^{\alpha},

for
\phi\in C_{0}^{\alpha}(\Omega),
which denotes the space of Hölder continuous functions. Finally, invoking Lemma
10
in the previous inequality, we obtain

\displaystyle\lvert<\tilde{\mathcal{L}}^{\Delta}_{1},\phi>\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}C(X,T)^{\frac{1}{3}}\mathcal{K}_{8}\lVert S_{u}\rVert\frac{\left(4XT\right)^{\frac{2}{3}}}{\lambda^{\frac{2}{3}}}\lVert\phi\rVert_{C_{0}^{\alpha}}\Delta x^{\alpha-\frac{2}{3}}

(7.17)

\displaystyle{\hskip 14.22636pt}+\frac{1}{2\lambda}\left(\lVert S_{k}\rVert T+\lVert S_{u}\rVert\lambda\lVert f_{k}\rVert T\right)\lVert k\rVert_{BV}\lVert\phi\rVert_{C_{0}^{\alpha}}\Delta x^{\alpha}\quad\mbox{for}\,\phi\in C_{0}^{\alpha}(\Omega).

Now, by the Sobolev’s imbedding theorem (see
[
1
]
) we have the inclusion
W_{0}^{1,p}(\Omega)\hookrightarrow C_{0}^{\alpha}(\Omega)
for
\alpha\in\left(0,1-\frac{2}{p}\right].
Consequently, for a fixed
\alpha_{1}\in(2/3,1)
and
p_{1}=\frac{2}{1-\alpha_{1}},
there exists a constant
\mathcal{K}_{p_{1}}>0,
such that

\displaystyle\lVert\phi\rVert_{C_{0}^{\alpha_{1}}(\Omega)}\leq\mathcal{K}_{p_{1}}\lVert\phi\rVert_{W_{0}^{1,p_{1}}(\Omega)}\,\,\forall\,\,\phi\in W_{0}^{1,p_{1}}(\Omega).

(7.18)

Using (
7.18
) in (
7.17
) amounts to

\displaystyle\lvert<\tilde{\mathcal{L}}^{\Delta}_{1},\phi>\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}C(X,T)^{\frac{1}{3}}\mathcal{K}_{p_{1}}\mathcal{K}_{8}\lVert S_{u}\rVert\frac{\left(4XT\right)^{\frac{2}{3}}}{\lambda^{\frac{2}{3}}}\lVert\phi\rVert_{W_{0}^{1,p_{1}}(\Omega)}\Delta x^{\alpha_{1}-\frac{2}{3}}

(7.19)

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2\lambda}\mathcal{K}_{p_{1}}\left(\lVert S_{k}\rVert T+\lVert S_{u}\rVert\lambda\lVert f_{k}\rVert T\right)\lVert k\rVert_{BV}\lVert\phi\rVert_{W_{0}^{1,p_{1}}(\Omega)}\Delta x^{\alpha_{1}}.

This implies that

\displaystyle\lim_{\Delta\rightarrow 0}\lVert\tilde{\mathcal{L}}^{\Delta}_{1}\rVert_{\mathrm{W}^{-1,q_{1}}(\Omega)}\leq\lim_{\Delta\rightarrow 0}(\mathcal{K}_{9}\Delta x^{\alpha_{1}-\frac{2}{3}}+\mathcal{K}_{10}\Delta x^{\alpha_{1}})=0\quad\mbox{for}\quad q_{1}=\frac{2}{1+\alpha_{1}}.

(7.20)

where

\displaystyle\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{9}

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{2}C^{\frac{1}{3}}\mathcal{K}_{p_{1}}\mathcal{K}_{8}\lVert S_{u}\rVert\frac{\left(4XT\right)^{\frac{2}{3}}}{\lambda^{\frac{2}{3}}},\,\mathcal{K}_{10}:=\frac{1}{2\lambda}\mathcal{K}_{p_{1}}\left(\lVert S_{k}\rVert T+\lVert S_{u}\rVert\lambda\lVert f_{k}\rVert T\right)\lVert k\rVert_{BV}.

From this, we conclude that
\{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}^{\Delta}_{1}\}_{\Delta>0}
is compact in
\mathrm{W}^{-1,q_{1}}(\Omega),
for
\displaystyle q_{1}=\frac{2}{1+\alpha_{1}}\in(1,2).
In an entirely analogous way, we obtain that the set
\{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}^{\Delta}_{2}\}_{\Delta>0}
is compact in
\mathrm{W}^{-1,q_{1}}(\Omega).

Next, we consider the term
<\tilde{\mathcal{L}}^{\Delta}_{4,2},\phi>
from (
7.8
), and apply summation by parts to write

\displaystyle<\tilde{\mathcal{L}}^{\Delta}_{4,2},\phi>

\displaystyle=\lambda\Delta x\sum_{n=0}^{N-2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lvert j\rvert\leq J\\
j+\frac{n+1}{2}\in\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}\left(\phi_{{j-\frac{1}{2}}}^{n+1}-\phi_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n+1}\right)S_{u}(k_{j},u_{j}^{n})(f(k_{j},u_{j}^{n})-g(k_{j},u_{j}^{n})).

(7.21)

From the expression (
6.21
) in the proof of Lemma
10
, we deduce that

\displaystyle\lambda\lvert f(k_{j},u_{j}^{n})-g(k_{j},u_{j}^{n})\rvert

\displaystyle=\lambda\Big\lvert\frac{\lambda}{2}(a_{j}^{n})^{2}\sigma_{j}^{n}-\frac{1}{8}(\lambda a_{j}^{n}\sigma_{j}^{n})^{2}f_{uu}(k_{j},\zeta_{2})-\frac{1}{8\lambda}\sigma_{j}^{n}\Big\rvert

(7.22)

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{K}_{11}\lvert\Delta u_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}\rvert,

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{11}:=\frac{\kappa^{2}}{2}+\frac{1}{4}C_{u_{0}}\kappa^{2}\gamma_{2}\lambda+\frac{1}{8}.

Inserting the estimate (
7.22
) into (
7.21
) and observing that
\lambda\lVert S_{u}\rVert\leq\lambda\lVert f_{u}\rVert\leq\kappa,
we apply Hölder’s inequality on (
7.21
) along with the cubic estimate (
10
) of Lemma
10
, to obtain

\displaystyle|\langle\tilde{\mathcal{L}}^{\Delta}_{4,2},\phi\rangle|

\displaystyle\leq\kappa\mathcal{K}_{11}\left(\Delta x\sum_{n=0}^{N-2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lvert j\rvert\leq J\\
j+\frac{n+1}{2}\in\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}|\Delta\phi_{j}^{n}|^{3/2}\right)^{2/3}\left(\Delta x\sum_{n=0}^{N-2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lvert j\rvert\leq J\\
j+\frac{n+1}{2}\in\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}|\Delta u_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}|^{3}\right)^{1/3}

(7.23)

\displaystyle\leq\kappa\mathcal{K}_{11}C(X,T)^{\frac{1}{3}}\left(\Delta x^{(1+\frac{3}{2}\alpha)}\lVert\phi\rVert_{C^{\alpha}_{0}}^{\frac{3}{2}\alpha}\sum_{n=0}^{N-2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lvert j\rvert\leq J\\
j+\frac{n+1}{2}\in\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}1\right)^{2/3}

\displaystyle\leq\kappa\mathcal{K}_{11}C(X,T)^{\frac{1}{3}}||\phi||_{C^{\alpha}_{0}}(\Delta x)^{\alpha+\frac{2}{3}}\frac{\left(4XT\right)^{\frac{2}{3}}}{\lambda^{\frac{2}{3}}(\Delta x)^{\frac{4}{3}}}\leq\mathcal{K}_{12}||\phi||_{C^{\alpha}_{0}}(\Delta x)^{\alpha-\frac{2}{3}}.

for
\phi\in C_{0}^{\alpha}(\Omega)
, where
\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{12}:=\kappa\mathcal{K}_{11}C(X,T)^{\frac{1}{3}}\left(\frac{4XT}{\lambda}\right)^{\frac{2}{3}}
with
C(X,T)
as in Lemma
10
. To get the penultimate inequality in the above estimate, we have used the facts that
X+\Delta x\leq J\Delta x\leq X+2\Delta x
and
(N-1)\Delta x\leq T.

Now, arguments similar to those in (
7.17
), (
7.19
) and (
7.20
), give compactness of
\{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}^{\Delta}_{4,2}\}_{\Delta>0}
in
\mathrm{W}^{-1,q_{1}}(\Omega),
for the same
q_{1}\in(1,2)
as in the case of
\tilde{\mathcal{L}}^{\Delta}_{1}
and
\tilde{\mathcal{L}}^{\Delta}_{2}.

Next, we consider
\{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}^{\Delta}_{3}\},
and obtain an estimate on the term
{<\tilde{\mathcal{L}}^{\Delta}_{3},\phi>}
using the observation

\displaystyle\lvert Q_{j+\frac{1}{2}}^{n-1}-Q_{j-\frac{1}{2}}^{n-1}\rvert\leq\lVert Q_{k}\rVert\lvert k_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}-k_{{j-\frac{1}{2}}}\rvert+\lVert Q_{u}\rVert\lvert\Delta u_{j}^{n}\rvert,

as follows

\displaystyle\lvert<\tilde{\mathcal{L}}^{\Delta}_{3},\phi>\rvert

(7.24)

\displaystyle\leq\lambda\lVert\phi\rVert_{C_{0}^{\alpha}}(\Delta x)^{\alpha+1}\lVert Q_{k}\rVert\lVert k\rVert_{BV}\left(\frac{2T}{\lambda\Delta x}\right)

\displaystyle{\hskip 14.22636pt}+\lambda\lVert\phi\rVert_{C_{0}^{\alpha}}(\Delta x)^{\alpha+\frac{2}{3}}\lVert Q_{u}\rVert\left(\Delta x\sum_{n=1}^{N-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lvert j\rvert\leq J\\
j+\frac{n}{2}\in\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}\lvert\Delta u_{j}^{n-1}\rvert^{3}\right)^{\frac{1}{3}}\left(\sum_{n=1}^{N-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lvert j\rvert\leq J\\
j+\frac{n}{2}\in\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}1\right)^{\frac{2}{3}}

\displaystyle\leq 2\lVert\phi\rVert_{C_{0}^{\alpha}}(\Delta x)^{\alpha}\lVert Q_{k}\rVert\lVert k\rVert_{BV}T+\lambda C^{\frac{1}{3}}\lVert\phi\rVert_{C_{0}^{\alpha}}(\Delta x)^{\alpha+\frac{2}{3}}\lVert Q_{u}\rVert\left(\frac{4XT}{\lambda(\Delta x)^{2}}\right)^{\frac{2}{3}}.

Now, with the same arguments leading to (
7.20
), we conclude that the set
\{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}^{\Delta}_{3}\}_{\Delta>0}
is compact in
\mathrm{W}^{-1,q_{1}}(\Omega)

for the same
q_{1}\in(1,2)
as in the case of
\tilde{\mathcal{L}}^{\Delta}_{1},

\tilde{\mathcal{L}}^{\Delta}_{2}
and
\tilde{\mathcal{L}}^{\Delta}_{4,2}.

By summarizing Steps 1 and 2, we conclude that the collection
\{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}^{\Delta}\}_{\Delta>0}
is compact in
\mathrm{W}^{-1,q_{1}}(\Omega),
where
q_{1}\in(1,2)
is as given in Step 2.
Furthermore, combining this result with (
7.2
), we deduce that

\displaystyle\{\mathcal{L}^{\Delta}\}_{\Delta>0}\,\mbox{is compact in}\,\mathrm{W}^{-1,q}(\Omega),\,\mbox{ for }\,q=q_{1}\in(1,2).

(7.25)

Now, owing to the
\mathrm{L}^{\infty}
boundedness (
5.3
) of the approximate solutions
u_{\Delta}
(Theorem
8
), it is straightforward to see that
\{\mathcal{L}^{\Delta}\}
is bounded in
\mathrm{W}^{-1,r}(\Omega)
for any
r>2.
Finally, applying the the interpolation result Lemma
7
we conclude that

\{\mathcal{L}^{\Delta}\}_{\Delta>0}\,\mbox{is compact in}\,\mathrm{W}^{-1,2}(\Omega).

Since
\Omega
is an arbitrary bounded open subset, it follows that
\{\mathcal{L}^{\Delta}\}_{\Delta>0}
is compact in
\mathrm{W}^{-1,2}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}).
This completes the proof.

We now present the weak solution convergence result for the proposed scheme (
3.4
), in the following theorem.

Theorem 13
.

Let the initial datum
u_{0}\in(\mathrm{L}^{\infty}\cap\mathrm{BV})(\mathbb{R})
and
\{u_{\Delta}\}_{\Delta>0}
be the approximate solutions (
3.8
) obtained from the second-order scheme (
3.4
) under the CFL condition (
6.3
). Then, there exists a subsequence
\{\Delta_{m}\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
with
\displaystyle\lim_{m\to\infty}\Delta_{m}=0
such that
\{u_{\Delta_{m}}\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
converges strongly to a weak solution of the problem (
1.1
). i.e.,

\displaystyle u_{\Delta_{m}}\xrightarrow{\Delta_{m}\to 0}u\,\,\mbox{in}\,\,\mathrm{L}^{p}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R^{+}})\,\,\,\mbox{for any}\,\,p\in[1,\infty)\,\,\mbox{and a.e. in}\,\,\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}.

Proof.

Consider a sequence
\displaystyle\{\Delta_{m}\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
such that
\displaystyle\lim_{m\rightarrow\infty}\Delta_{m}=0.
Now, Theorem
8
and Lemma
12
allow us to use the compensated compactness result from Theorem
6
for the sequence
\{u_{\Delta_{m}}\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}.
Hence, there exists a subsequence, again denoted by
\displaystyle\{\Delta_{m}\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}},
such that
u_{\Delta_{m}}\rightarrow u
pointwise a.e., as
m\rightarrow\infty
. Therefore, for any
p\in[1,\infty),
it follows that
\lvert u_{\Delta_{m}}-u\rvert^{p}\rightarrow 0
as
\Delta_{m}\rightarrow 0,
pointwise a.e.. Since the approximate solutions satisfy the
\mathrm{L}^{\infty}
-estimate
\lVert u_{\Delta}\rVert\leq C_{u_{0}}
by Theorem
8
and the limit
u\in\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R_{+}})
(by Theorem
6
), it follows that
\lvert u_{\Delta}-u\rvert^{p}\leq(C_{u_{0}}+\lVert u\rVert)^{p}.
Consequently, applying the dominated convergence theorem, for any compact set
K\subseteq\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}_{+},
we have

\displaystyle

\displaystyle\lim_{\Delta\rightarrow 0}\lVert u_{\Delta_{m}}-u\rVert^{p}_{\mathrm{L}^{p}(K)}=0,\,\mbox{or equivalently,}

(7.26)

\displaystyle u_{\Delta_{m}}\xrightarrow{\Delta_{m}\rightarrow 0}u\,\mbox{in}\,\mathrm{L}^{p}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}_{+})\,\mbox{for any}\,p\in[1,+\infty).

Finally, employing a Lax-Wendroff type argument
[
42
]
which uses the the
\mathrm{L}^{\infty}
- boundedness of
\{u_{\Delta}\}_{\Delta>0}
and the strong convergence
u_{\Delta_{m}}\xrightarrow{}u
from (
7.26
), we can show that the limit
u
is a weak solution of (
1.1
). This completes the proof.

8
Convergence to the entropy solution

In order to show the entropy convergence, we follow the approach of
[
62
,
64
,
65
]
. As the first step in this framework, we derive an entropy-convergence result for numerical schemes in the predictor-corrector form:

\displaystyle u^{n+1}_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}=\bar{u}^{n+1}_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}-a_{{j+1}}^{n+1}+a_{j}^{n+1},

approximating (
1.1
). This result builds on the idea of Theorem 3.1 in
[
64
]
, incorporating essential modifications to address the discontinuous flux case, as detailed in the following theorem.

Theorem 14
.

Suppose that a numerical scheme approximating (
1.1
) can be written in the form:

u^{n+1}_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}=\bar{u}^{n+1}_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}-a_{{j+1}}^{n+1}+a_{j}^{n+1},

(8.1)

where

(i)
\{\bar{u}_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n+1}\}_{{j\in\mathbb{Z}}}
is computed from
\{u_{j}^{n}\}_{{j\in\mathbb{Z}}}
using the Lax-Friedrichs scheme (
3.10
) as:

\displaystyle\bar{u}^{n+1}_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}(u^{n}_{j}+u^{n}_{j+1})-\lambda\left(f(k_{j+1},u^{n}_{j+1})-f(k_{j},u^{n}_{j})\right),

(8.2)

(ii)
|a^{n+1}_{j}|\leq\mathcal{K}{\Delta x}^{\alpha},

{j\in\mathbb{Z}}
for some constant
\mathcal{K}>0
which is independent of
\Delta x
and for some
\alpha\in(\frac{2}{3},1),

(iii) The approximate solutions
u_{\Delta}
obtained from the predictor-corrector scheme is bounded in the
\mathrm{L}^{\infty}
-norm, and for any fixed
T>0,X>0,
with
N:=\lfloor T/\Delta t\rfloor+1
and
J:=\lfloor X/\Delta x\rfloor+1,
satisfies an estimate of the form

\displaystyle\Delta x\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lvert j\rvert\leq J\\
j+\frac{n}{2}\in\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}\lvert\Delta u_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}\rvert^{3}\leq C,

(8.3)

for a constant
C
independent of
\Delta x
and converges pointwise a.e. to a function
u\in\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}).

Then the limit
u
of the approximate solutions
u_{\Delta}
generated by the scheme (
8.1
) is the entropy solution (
2.5
) to the problem (
1.1
).

Proof.

The first-order Lax-Friedrichs scheme (
3.10
) used to obtain
\bar{u}^{n+1}_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}
satisfies a discrete cell entropy inequality (see
[
37
]
for more details) given by:

\displaystyle\lvert\bar{u}^{n+1}_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}-c\rvert-\frac{1}{2}\lvert u^{n}_{j+1}-c\rvert

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\lvert u^{n}_{j}-c\rvert+\lambda\left(F(k_{{j+1}},u_{{j+1}}^{n},c)-F(k_{j},u_{j}^{n},c)\right)

\displaystyle{\hskip 14.22636pt}-\lambda\sign(\bar{u}^{n+1}_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}-c)(f(k_{{j+1}},c)-f(k_{j},c))\leq 0,

for
F(k,u,c):=\mathrm{sgn}(u-c)(f(k,u)-f(k,c)).
This in turn implies that

\displaystyle\lvert\bar{u}^{n+1}_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}-c\rvert-\frac{1}{2}\lvert u^{n}_{j+1}-c\rvert-\frac{1}{2}\lvert u^{n}_{j}-c\rvert

\displaystyle+\lambda\left(F(k_{{j+1}},u_{{j+1}}^{n},c)-F(k_{j},u_{j}^{n},c)\right)

(8.4)

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle-\lambda\lvert f(k_{{j+1}},c)-f(k_{j},c)\rvert\leq 0.

Now, consider a non-negative test function
\phi
with
\mathrm{supp}(\phi)\subseteq[-X,X]\times[0,T],
let
N=\lfloor T/\Delta t\rfloor+1,

J=\lfloor X/\Delta x\rfloor+1
and denote
\phi_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}:=\phi(x_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}},t^{n}).
Adding
\lvert u^{n+1}_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}-c\rvert
to either side of (
8.4
), multiplying it with
\Delta x\phi_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}
and summing over
n=0,1,\dots,N-1
and
j+\frac{n}{2}\in\mathbb{Z}
with
\lvert j\rvert\leq J,
we obtain the following inequality

\displaystyle\Delta x\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lvert j\rvert\leq J\\
j+\frac{n}{2}\in\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}\phi_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}\lvert u^{n+1}_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}-c\rvert-\frac{1}{2}\Delta x\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lvert j\rvert\leq J\\
j+\frac{n}{2}\in\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}\phi_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}\lvert u^{n}_{j+1}-c\rvert

(8.5)

\displaystyle{\hskip 14.22636pt}-\frac{1}{2}\Delta x\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lvert j\rvert\leq J\\
j+\frac{n}{2}\in\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}\phi_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}\lvert u^{n}_{j}-c\rvert-\Delta x\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lvert j\rvert\leq J\\
j+\frac{n}{2}\in\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}\phi_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}\lambda\lvert f(k_{{j+1}},c)-f(k_{j},c)\rvert

\displaystyle{\hskip 14.22636pt}+\Delta x\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lvert j\rvert\leq J\\
j+\frac{n}{2}\in\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}\phi_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}\lambda\left(F(k_{{j+1}},u_{{j+1}}^{n},c)-F(k_{j},u_{j}^{n},c)\right)\leq\mathcal{J},

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}

\displaystyle:=\Delta x\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lvert j\rvert\leq J\\
j+\frac{n}{2}\in\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}\phi_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}\left(\lvert u^{n+1}_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}-c\rvert-\lvert\bar{u}^{n+1}_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}-c\rvert\right).

First, we show that the term
\mathcal{J}\rightarrow 0
as
\Delta\rightarrow 0.
For this, we consider the function
\displaystyle S(u)=\begin{cases}\frac{u^{2}}{2}+\frac{1}{2},&\text{if }|u|<1,\\
|u|,&\text{if }|u|\geq 1\end{cases}
and for a fixed
c\in\mathbb{R}
and
\epsilon>0,
we define
\displaystyle S_{\epsilon}(u)=\epsilon S(\frac{u-c}{\epsilon}).
It is easy to see that
S_{\epsilon}\rightarrow\lvert u-c\rvert
as
\epsilon\rightarrow 0,
uniformly in the supremum norm. In particular,

\displaystyle S_{\epsilon}(u)-\lvert u-c\rvert

\displaystyle=0,\quad\,\,\,\,\mbox{if}\,\,\lvert u-c\rvert\geq\epsilon,\,\,\mbox{and}

(8.6)

\displaystyle\big\lvert S_{\epsilon}(u)-\lvert u-c\rvert\big\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\frac{3}{2}\epsilon,\quad\mbox{if}\,\,\lvert u-c\rvert<\epsilon.

Upon adding and subtracting suitable terms,
\mathcal{J}
can be written as

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{J}_{0}+\mathcal{J}_{1}+\mathcal{J}_{2},

(8.7)

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}_{0}

\displaystyle:=\Delta x\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lvert j\rvert\leq J\\
j+\frac{n}{2}\in\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}\phi_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}\left(S_{\epsilon}(u^{n+1}_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}})-S_{\epsilon}(\bar{u}^{n+1}_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}})\right),

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}_{1}

\displaystyle:=\Delta x\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lvert j\rvert\leq J\\
j+\frac{n}{2}\in\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}\phi_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}\left(\lvert u^{n+1}_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}-c\rvert-S_{\epsilon}(u^{n+1}_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}})\right),

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}_{2}

\displaystyle:=-\Delta x\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lvert j\rvert\leq J\\
j+\frac{n}{2}\in\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}\phi_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}\left(\lvert\bar{u}^{n+1}_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}-c\rvert-S_{\epsilon}(\bar{u}^{n+1}_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}})\right).

Now, (
8.6
) implies that for
\epsilon\leq\Delta t^{2},

\displaystyle\lvert\mathcal{J}_{1}\rvert,\lvert\mathcal{J}_{2}\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\frac{3}{2}\epsilon\Delta x\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lvert j\rvert\leq J\\
j+\frac{n}{2}\in\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}\lVert\phi\rVert\leq\frac{3}{2}\Delta t\lVert\phi\rVert(8XT),

and hence

\displaystyle\lim_{\Delta\rightarrow 0}\mathcal{J}_{1}=\lim_{\Delta\rightarrow 0}\mathcal{J}_{2}=0.

(8.8)

Further, the convexity of
S_{\epsilon}
gives us

\displaystyle S_{\epsilon}(u^{n+1}_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}})-S_{\epsilon}(\bar{u}^{n+1}_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}})\leq S_{\epsilon}^{\prime}(u^{n+1}_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}})\left(u^{n+1}_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}-\bar{u}^{n+1}_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}\right)=S_{\epsilon}^{\prime}(u^{n+1}_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}})\left(a^{n+1}_{j}-a^{n+1}_{{j+1}}\right).

(8.9)

Using (
8.9
) in
\mathcal{J}_{0}
and applying summation by parts, we obtain the following inequality

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}_{0}

\displaystyle\leq\Delta x\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lvert j\rvert\leq J\\
j+\frac{n}{2}\in\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}a^{n+1}_{{j+1}}\left(\phi_{j+\frac{3}{2}}^{n}S_{\epsilon}^{\prime}(u^{n+1}_{j+\frac{3}{2}})-\phi_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}S_{\epsilon}^{\prime}(u^{n+1}_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}})\right).

(8.10)

Now, adding and subtracting the term
\Delta xa_{{j+1}}^{n+1}\phi_{j+\frac{3}{2}}^{n}S_{\epsilon}^{\prime}(u^{n+1}_{j+\frac{1}{2}})
inside the summation in the RHS of (
8.10
), we have
\mathcal{J}_{0}\leq\mathcal{J}_{0}^{a}+\mathcal{J}_{0}^{b},
where

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}_{0}^{a}

\displaystyle:=\Delta x\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lvert j\rvert\leq J\\
j+\frac{n}{2}\in\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}a^{n+1}_{{j+1}}\phi_{j+\frac{3}{2}}^{n}\left(S_{\epsilon}^{\prime}(u^{n+1}_{j+\frac{3}{2}})-S_{\epsilon}^{\prime}(u^{n+1}_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}})\right),

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}_{0}^{b}

\displaystyle:=\Delta x\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lvert j\rvert\leq J\\
j+\frac{n}{2}\in\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}a^{n+1}_{{j+1}}\left(\phi_{j+\frac{3}{2}}^{n}-\phi_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}\right)S_{\epsilon}^{\prime}(u^{n+1}_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}).

Using the assumption that
\lvert a^{n+1}_{{j+1}}\rvert\leq\mathcal{K}\Delta x^{\alpha}
for
{j\in\mathbb{Z}},
along with the condition
\mathrm{supp}(\phi)\subseteq[-X,X]\times[0,T],
we apply Hölder’s inequality, followed by (
8.3
), to obtain the following estimate for
\mathcal{J}_{0}^{a}
:

\displaystyle\lvert\mathcal{J}_{0}^{a}\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{K}(\Delta x)^{\alpha}\lVert\phi\rVert\lVert S_{\epsilon}^{\prime\prime}\rVert\left(\Delta x\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lvert j\rvert\leq J\\
j+\frac{n}{2}\in\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}\lvert\Delta u^{n+1}_{j+1}\rvert^{3}\right)^{\frac{1}{3}}\left(\Delta x\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lvert j\rvert\leq J\\
j+\frac{n}{2}\in\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}1\right)^{\frac{2}{3}}

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{K}(\Delta x)^{\alpha}\lVert\phi\rVert\lVert S_{\epsilon}^{\prime\prime}\rVert C^{\frac{1}{3}}\left(\frac{8XT}{\Delta t}\right)^{\frac{2}{3}}\leq\frac{1}{\lambda^{\frac{2}{3}}}\mathcal{K}(\Delta x)^{\alpha-\frac{2}{3}}\lVert\phi\rVert\lVert S_{\epsilon}^{\prime\prime}\rVert C^{\frac{1}{3}}\left(8XT\right)^{\frac{2}{3}}.

Now, since
\alpha>\frac{2}{3}
by assumption, it follows that
\lvert\mathcal{J}_{0}^{a}\rvert\rightarrow 0
as
\Delta\rightarrow 0.

Next, it is straightforward to see that

\displaystyle\lvert\mathcal{J}_{0}^{b}\rvert

\displaystyle=\Big\lvert\frac{1}{\lambda}\Delta x\Delta t\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lvert j\rvert\leq J\\
j+\frac{n}{2}\in\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}a^{n+1}_{{j+1}}\left(\frac{\phi_{j+\frac{3}{2}}^{n}-\phi_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}^{n}}{\Delta x}\right)S_{\epsilon}^{\prime}(u^{n+1}_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}})\Big\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\lambda}\mathcal{K}(\Delta x)^{\alpha}\lVert\phi_{x}\rVert\lVert S_{\epsilon}^{\prime}\rVert 8XT,

by which we conclude that
\lvert\mathcal{J}_{0}^{b}\rvert\rightarrow 0
as
\Delta\rightarrow 0.
Therefore,
\displaystyle\lim_{\Delta\rightarrow 0}\mathcal{J}_{0}\leq 0.
This, together with (
8.8
) applied on (
8.7
) imply that

\displaystyle\displaystyle\lim_{\Delta\rightarrow 0}\mathcal{J}\leq 0.

(8.11)

Finally, using (
8.11
) and proceeding as in the proofs of Lemmas 5.3 and 5.4 from
[
37
]
, it is easy to show from (
8.5
) that

\displaystyle\int\int_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}(|u-c|\phi_{t}+\sign(u-c)(f(k,u)-f(k,c))\phi_{x})\,\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\,\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t

(8.12)

\displaystyle{\hskip 14.22636pt}+\int_{\mathbb{R}}|u_{0}-c|\phi(x,0)\,\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x+\int\int_{(\mathbb{R}\setminus D)\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}|f(k(x),c)_{x}|\phi\,\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\,\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle{\hskip 14.22636pt}+\sum_{m=1}^{M}\int_{0}^{\infty}|f(k^{+}_{m},c)-f(k^{-}_{m},c)|\phi(x_{m},t)\,\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t\geq 0,

where
D
is as in
(H6)
. This concludes the proof.

Remark 15
.

We emphasize that the entropy convergence result in Theorem 3.1 of
[
64
]
is based on a
\mathrm{BV}
-estimate for the approximate solutions. However, for conservation laws with discontinuous coefficients,
\mathrm{BV}
-estimates need not be available in general (see
[
2
]
). In this context, as a key novelty of our approach, we show in Theorem
14
that a significantly weaker cubic estimate of the form (
6.4
) is sufficient to establish the desired entropy convergence.

Now, our strategy essentially is to use Theorem
14
to prove the convergence of the proposed second-order scheme (
3.4
) to the entropy solution. To this end, we note that the scheme (
3.4
) can be written in the predictor-corrector form

\displaystyle\bar{u}^{n+1}_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}(u^{n}_{j}+u^{n}_{j+1})-\lambda\left(f(k_{j+1},u^{n}_{j+1})-f(k_{j},u^{n}_{j})\right),

(8.13)

\displaystyle u^{n+1}_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}

\displaystyle=\bar{u}^{n+1}_{{j+\frac{1}{2}}}-a_{{j+1}}^{n+1}+a_{j}^{n+1},

with the correction terms

\displaystyle a_{j}^{n+1}

\displaystyle:=\lambda\left(f(k_{j},u_{j}^{n+\frac{1}{2}})-f(k_{j},u_{j}^{n})\right)+\frac{1}{8}\sigma_{j}^{n},

(8.14)

\displaystyle=\left(-\frac{\lambda^{2}}{2}f_{u}(k_{j},\zeta_{j})f_{u}(k_{j},u_{j}^{n})+\frac{1}{8}\right)\sigma_{j}^{n},

for some
\zeta_{j}\in\mathcal{I}(u_{j}^{n},u_{j}^{{n+\frac{1}{2}}}).
Next, we modify the scheme (
8.13
) by redefining the slopes (
3.2
) as

\displaystyle

\displaystyle\sigma_{j}^{n}

(8.15)

\displaystyle=\mbox{ minmod}\left((u^{n}_{j+1}-u^{n}_{j}),\frac{1}{2}(u^{n}_{j+1}-u^{n}_{j-1}),(u^{n}_{j}-u^{n}_{j-1}),\sign(u^{n}_{{j+1}}-u^{n}_{j})\tilde{\mathcal{K}}(\Delta x)^{\alpha}\right),

for some constants
\tilde{\mathcal{K}}>0
and
\alpha\in(\frac{2}{3},1).

With this modification, it is direct to see from (
8.14
) that the correction terms
\{a_{j}^{n+1}\}_{{j\in\mathbb{Z}}}
in (
8.13
) satisfy the estimate

\displaystyle\lvert a_{j}^{n+1}\rvert\leq\mathcal{K}(\Delta x)^{\alpha},

(8.16)

for
{j\in\mathbb{Z}},
where
\displaystyle\mathcal{K}:=\left(\frac{1}{2}\lambda^{2}\lVert f_{u}\rVert^{2}+\frac{1}{8}\right)\tilde{\mathcal{K}}.

Finally, we conclude this section with the entropy convergence result, stated below.

Theorem 16
.

Let the initial datum
u_{0}
be such that
u_{0}\in(\mathrm{L}^{\infty}\cap\mathrm{BV})(\mathbb{R}),
with
\hbox{\hskip 2.86229pt\hskip-2.86229pt\hbox{$u$}\hskip-2.86229pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-1.0pt}{\hbox{\rule{3.44444pt}{0.32289pt}}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.86229pt}\leq u_{0}(x)\leq\bar{u},
for
x\in\mathbb{R}.
Under the CFL condition (
6.3
) and hypotheses
(H1)
-
(H7)
, the approximate solutions
\{u_{\Delta}\}_{\Delta>0}
(
3.8
) obtained from the scheme (
3.4
) with the modified slopes (
8.15
) converge to the entropy solution (
2.5
) of the problem (
1.1
).

Proof.

Since the second-order scheme (
3.4
) with the modified slopes (
8.15
) can be reformulated in the predictor-corrector form, it suffices to show that the hypotheses (i)-(iii) of Theorem
14
hold true. From (
8.13
), it is clear that the predictor step employs the Lax-Friedrichs time-stepping, thereby confirming the validity of condition (i) in Theorem
14
. From (
8.16
), it follows that with the slope modification (
8.15
), the hypothesis (ii) of Theorem
14
also holds. Finally, observing that the
\mathrm{L}^{\infty}
- estimate (
5.3
) (Theorem
8
), the cubic estimate (
6.4
) (Lemma
10
), the
\mathrm{W}^{-1,2}_{\mathrm{loc}}
compactness result (Lemma
12
), and the convergence theorem (Theorem
13
) remain valid with the modified slopes (
8.15
), it follows that hypothesis (iii) is also satisfied. Now, an application of Theorem
14
yields the desired convergence to the entropy solution.

Remark 17
.

In the implementation of the numerical scheme, the slope modification (
8.15
) is not really required. This is because, for any given mesh-size
\Delta x,
we can choose
\tilde{K}>0
large enough so that the modified slope (
8.15
) reduces to (
3.2
). In particular, for mesh sizes
\Delta x\geq\epsilon
for some fixed
\epsilon>0,
we can choose
\tilde{K}=2C_{u_{0}}\epsilon^{-\alpha},
where
C_{u_{0}}
is as given in (
5.3
). For more details, see
[
43
]
(p. 158, below Eq. (26)),
[
64
]
(p. 68, below Fig. 3) and
[
63
]
(p. 577, Remark).

9
Numerical experiments

In this section, we present numerical experiments to illustrate the performance of the second-order scheme (
3.4
) in comparison to the first-order Lax-Friedrichs scheme (
3.10
). In the following, we denote the second-order scheme (
3.4
) by SO and the Lax-Friedrichs scheme (
3.10
) by LF. Alluding to Remark
1
, we focus on examples where the flux function is strictly concave, as these are the types of fluxes most commonly considered in the literature. We divide the computational domain into cells of size
\Delta x.
The CFL condition (
6.1
), required for proving the convergence of the SO scheme, is highly restrictive and need not be optimal. Therefore, to compute the time-step
\Delta t,
we impose the less restrictive CFL condition (
5.1
) (which ensures the maximum principle). In each test case, both the LF and SO solutions are computed with the time-step corresponding to the SO scheme. Also, we apply absorbing boundary conditions in both the examples.

Example 9.18
.

We consider an example from
[
34
]
, where the flux function in (
1.1
) is given by:

\displaystyle f(k,u)

\displaystyle=ku(1-u),\quad k(x)

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}3\,\,\,\mbox{if}\,x<0,\\
1\,\,\,\mbox{if}\,x\geq 0.\end{cases}

(9.1)

The discontinuous flux (
9.1
) is depicted in Figure
1
(a), where
f_{l}(u)=3u(1-u)
and
f_{r}(u)=u(1-u).

9.18

(b)

9.19

:
The fluxes to the left (
f_{l}
) and right (
f_{r}
) of
x=0.

Note that there is no flux crossing in this case and the crossing condition
(H7)
holds trivially for (
9.1
). We set the initial condition as the constant function

\displaystyle u_{0}(x)=0.15,

(9.2)

and compute the numerical solutions in the domain
[-1,1]
with a mesh of size
\Delta x=2/50
at the time levels
t\in\{0.8,1.6\}.
Here, the reference solutions are computed with the LF scheme using a fine mesh of size
\Delta x=2/1000.
The results are displayed in Figure

. We observe that the SO scheme exhibits lower numerical diffusion and provides a more accurate approximation of the solution, especially near the discontinuities.

]
, where the flux function in (

) is of the form

\displaystyle f(H(x),u(x,t))

\displaystyle=H(x)f_{r}(u)+(1-H(x))f_{l}(u)=\begin{cases}f_{l}(u)\quad\mbox{for}\,x<0,\\
f_{r}(u)\quad\mbox{for}\,x\geq 0,\end{cases}

(9.3)

with

\displaystyle f_{l}(u):=\frac{2u(1-u)}{1+u},\quad f_{r}(u):=\frac{2u(1-u)}{2-u}.

(9.4)

9.3
) is depicted in Figure

(b), where we note that the crossing condition
(H7)
is satisfied by (

\displaystyle u(x,0)=\begin{cases}0.9&\mbox{ if }x\leq 0,\\
0.2&\mbox{ otherwise.}\end{cases}

(9.5)

and compute the solutions using
\Delta x=8/50
at the time levels

and

(a) and

(b), respectively. The reference solutions are obtained with the LF scheme on a fine mesh of size
\Delta x=8/2000.

, the proposed SO scheme yields more accurate solutions, in comparison to the LF scheme.
